===== [p241-243] Chiller Maintenance > Preparation Tasks (4块) =====
--[p241]-- Preparation Tasks
1 Confirm that the equipment is in an idle state and change the process module to maintenance mode.
2 Press Temperature on the PM Maintenance screen to display the PM Temperature screen.
3 Set the upper electrode temperature to $4 0 ^ { \circ } \mathrm { C } .$
4 Confirm that the upper electrode temperature has dropped to 40 °C.
5 Confirm that Brine Temp of the top chiller has dropped to $4 0 ~ ^ { \circ } \mathrm { C }$ or lower.
--[p241]-- 6 Press Module on the PM Maintenance screen to display the PM Module screen.
7 Press Top Chiller on the PM Module screen to display the Control Top Chiller dialog.
8 Press Off on the Control Top Chiller dialog, and press OK to turn OFF the chiller to which you will be adding chiller coolant fluid.
TELTactrasVigusMaintenanceManualRev.3.2.6[LK-2]
9 Turn OFF the chiller main breaker, and lock out (see page 69).
--[p243]-- 6 Press Off on the Control Bottom Chiller dialog, and press OK to turn OFF the chiller to which you will be adding chiller coolant fluid.
TELTactrasVigusMaintenanceManualRev.3.2.6[LK-2]
7 Turn OFF the chiller main breaker, and lock out (see page 72).
--[p243]-- Preparation Tasks
1 Confirm that the equipment is in an idle state and change the process module to maintenance mode.
2 Press Temperature on the PM Maintenance screen to display the PM Temperature screen.
3 Check that Brine Temp of the bottom chiller has dropped to $4 0 ~ ^ { \circ } \mathrm { C }$ or less.
4 Press Module on the PM Maintenance screen to display the PM Module screen.
5 Press Bottom Chiller on the PM Module screen to display the Control Bottom Chiller dialog.

--[p202]-- Vaporized ethanol can be harmful to your health when wiping the target parts with a clean wipe moistened with ethanol.
Follow the conditions below when working. • The target part is at a normal temperature (approx. 25 °C).
• The amount of ethanol used to moisten the clean wipe must be 4 ml or less. • Keep distance of at least 20 cm from the wiping target to your nose or mouth.
• Wait at least 30 seconds after wiping when moving your face close to the target part (to check the conditions).
Wear a respirator for personal protective equipment when all the four conditions above are not satisfied.

Impacting the ESC (both the sides and the top where wafers are clamped) can decrease the effectiveness of the ESC to clamp wafers or cause the ESC to not be able to clamp wafers at all because of cracks, depressions, or exfoliation present on its surface. Take care not to impact the ESC (both the sides and the top where wafers are clamped) while servicing.

3 Use the following steps to attach the two view ports on the deposition shield:
Confirm that the attaching positions of the view ports are correct.
3.1 Attach the blank EPD window to the deposition shield.
3.2 Tighten the two blank EPD window securing screws to secure the blank EPD window. Torque specification: 0.05 N·m
--[p207]-- 4.2 Blow off the deposits and cleaning solvent remaining on the surface of the parts using the air gun, keeping a distance of at least 10 cm between the nozzle and the parts.
--[p187]-- 7.7 Press Close on the Drive Shutter dialog and press OK to close the shutter.
7.8 Turn OFF and lock out the sources of energy in the following order:

--[p204]-- 8.1 Set the air gun pressure to between 0.2–0.5 MPa.
--[p183]-- Equipment Damage

--[p206]-- NOTE Replace the cleaning solvent with fresh cleaning solvent after the solvent has been used to clean the equivalent of all the parts in 20 process chambers.
3.2 When the predetermined soaking time has passed, remove the parts from the solvent.

4 Remove the deposits and cleaning solvent remaining on the parts by doing the following:
4.1 Set the air gun pressure to between 0.2–0.5 MPa.
--[p199]-- Equipment Damage
Acid or alkaline cleaning solvents can corrode the parts you are cleaning. Do not use an acid or alkaline cleaning solvent for cleaning parts. Always use acetone or fluorinated solvent.
3.1 Confirm there are no deposits floating in the cleaning solvent and soak the part for 1– 12 hours. The amount of time required to clean the part depends on how much deposit is present. Always soak the parts the appropriate of time.
--[p219]-- Equipment Damage
Wiping the lifter pins with alcohol can cause the pins to swell, degrade lubricating ability and damage the equipment. Clean the lifter pins with a clean dry wipe.
13 Lightly wipe the components and the chamber walls with a clean wipe moistened with alcohol.

===== [p242-244] Chiller Maintenance > Equipment Damage (2块) =====
--[p242]-- Equipment Damage
If the chiller coolant fluid level exceeds the specification of the level gauge, the fluid in the chiller lines can not be fully retrieved when replenishing it, which can result in a leak. When adding chiller coolant fluid, make sure that the fluid level stays within the specification. Also, to avoid damaging the chiller or decreasing its efficiency, use the specified chiller coolant fluid only.

===== [p4-4] 1.1 Scope of Warranty (3块) =====
--[p4]-- Such maintenance shall be provided for the purpose of repair of the function and performance of the Products to the Specifications, and, at TEL’s discretion, if parts are required to be replaced, TEL shall replace such parts with new parts or recycled parts equivalent to the new parts in performance.
--[p4]-- In the event that the Products do not conform to the Specifications under the above conditions during the warranty period for the Products, TEL shall provide maintenance services for such warranted Products without charge.
--[p4]-- 1.1 Scope of Warranty
TOKYO ELECTRON LIMITED (“TEL”) shall warrant that certain items distributed by TEL (“Products”) will conform to specifications furnished or approved by TEL (“Specifications”) under normal use and service in accordance with the manuals which TEL provides with respect to the Products (“TEL’s manuals”).

===== [p47-47] Lockout and Tagout > WARNING (1块) =====

Unsafe conditions may be present for chemical leaks, crush hazards, and electrical shock. Lockout and tagout should only be performed by the trained and authorized personnel assigned to the work.

===== [p85-91] Preventive Maintenance Schedule > WARNING (2块) =====
--[p91]-- WARNING
--[p85]-- WARNING
Explosive/Equipment Damage
When the equipment continue operating without overhauling TMP for a prolonged term, the chemical reaction between the AI powder generated from the rotor contacting with the deposit resulted from the long term operation and the deposit powder leads to the abnormal combustion (detonation), and it could lead the major accident such as breaking TMP. Always perform the periodically TMP overhaul based on the scheduled maintenance list of this manual.
TELTactrasVigusMaintenanceManualRev.3.2.6[LK-2]

--[p101]-- Purpose:
To change the process module to independent maintenance mode after maintenance tasks are complete or before performing control system maintenance, and change to normal mode when the tasks are complete. To change the process module to independent maintenance mode, the process module must first be in maintenance mode.

===== [p241-244] Chiller Maintenance > Thermal Hazard (4块) =====
--[p244]-- Thermal Hazard
The chiller coolant fluid can be at a very high temperature or a very low temperature shortly after chiller operation. Direct contact could result in burns or frostbite. Always confirm the temperature of the chiller coolant fluid is above 20 °C and less than 40 °C on the control panel before servicing.
--[p242]-- Thermal Hazard
The chiller coolant fluid can be at a very high temperature or a very low temperature shortly after chiller operation. Direct contact could result in burns or frostbite. Always confirm the temperature of the chiller coolant fluid is above 20 °C and less than 40 °C on the control panel before servicing.
--[p243]-- Thermal Hazard
The chiller coolant fluid can be at a high temperature or a low temperature directly after chiller operation and direct contact can result in burns or frostbite. Always check the temperature of the chiller coolant fluid on the chiller operation screen before beginning work.
--[p241]-- Thermal Hazard
The chiller coolant fluid can be at a high temperature or a low temperature directly after chiller operation and direct contact can result in burns or frostbite. Always check the temperature of the chiller coolant fluid on the chiller operation screen before beginning work.

• The t arget part is at a normal t emperature (approx. 25 °C).
--[p191]-- Chemical
Residual hazardous material in the chamber and on parts can be harmful to your health. Wear appropriate personal protective equipment such as safety glasses and protective gloves when servicing. Wear a respirator if corrosive gases are used.

===== [p10-10] 4.1 What is Training Credit? (1块) =====
--[p10]-- 4.1 What is Training Credit?
For one unit of product purchased, the specified number of training credits is provided. One training credit allows one trainee to enroll in the Instructor-Led training for the product purchased for one day (during standard service hours). For Web-Based Training, a required number of training credits set per course allows one trainee to access to the learning content for 30 days.

--[p35]-- To perform the procedures included in this manual, you must fully understand the contents of this manual, the Basic Operations Manual, and the Advanced Operations Manual, and you must have successfully completed all the necessary training.
--[p35]-- This manual explains how to perform periodic maintenance on the TEL plasma etching system (hereafter called "this system" or "the system") and its support equipment to keep the system in good working condition.
This manual is intended for maintenance operators who have read and fully understand the contents of the Basic Operations Manual and the Advanced Operations Manual.

===== [p37-37] 1.7.1 Time Required to Perform Procedures <sub>0006M.20071101</sub> (2块) =====
--[p37]-- 1.7.1 Time Required to Perform Procedures <sub>0006M.20071101</sub>
The times given for procedures are representative of the amount of time required for each procedure and are based on the following conditions:
• The required tools, parts, and personnel are readily available.
• Personnel have successfully completed the appropriate training classes (example: TEA level II).
--[p37]-- The time given includes the time required to complete this procedure; however, the time does not include the time required to complete any referenced procedures.
• The time given does not take into account any local fab site policies.
• The procedures are performed as written without any omissions or revisions.
TELTactrasVigusMaintenanceManualRev.3.2.6[LK-2]
When a procedure is performed at a fab site, the actual time to perform the procedure may differ slightly from the time given in this manual.

===== [p237-239] 6.1 Replacing the Uninterruptible Power Supply (UPS) Battery <sub>16006M.20180901</sub> > Equipment Damage (7块) =====
--[p238]-- Equipment Damage
While attaching and removing the battery, use sufficient caution to prevent the battery from shortcircuiting.
7 Cover the battery-side part of the connector with vinyl tape for insulation.
--[p239]-- NOTE The UPS battery will be checked automatically and the BATTERY CONDITION LED (yellow) on the front of the UPS will flash. If no error is found after the UPS battery is checked for five seconds, the BATTERY CONDITION LED will be green. If the LED is flashing in orange, the battery is in abnormal state. Check the battery installation status.
11.4 Press and hold the Reset switch and Battery Check switch on the front of the UPS simultaneously for three seconds.
--[p239]-- Check the UPS battery and reset the UPS by doing the following:
11.1 Turn OFF all the breakers located on the right panel side of the AC power box except for CBU11.
11.2 Release the lockout of the AC power box main breaker and turn ON the power (see page 51).
11.3 From the back of the loader module on the orienter side, press the Machine On switch.
Verify that the buzzer sounds briefly and the RUN LED (green) in front of the UPS turns ON when the equipment power is tuned on.
--[p238]-- Equipment Damage
While attaching and removing the battery, use sufficient caution to prevent the battery from shortcircuiting.
9 Connect the battery connectors securely.
10 Attach the UPS front cover. If the front cover is secured with the wiring pinned underneath, there is a chance that the wiring can be cut. Confirm that the wiring is not pinned under the battery cover before securing it.
C h a p t e r 6 , A C P owe r B o x M a i n t e n a n c e
--[p239]-- 11.5 Press and hold the RESET switch for 10 seconds.
NOTE A buzzer in the UPS will sound for one second. If a buzzer does not   
sound, press and hold the RESET switch for 10 seconds.
11.6 Press and hold the RESET switch and BATTERY CHECK switch simultaneously for three seconds.
11.7 From the back of the loader module on the orienter side, press the Machine Off switch.
11.8 Turn OFF the main breaker(s). This action will cut the main power supply to the system.
11.9 Close the front panel of the AC power box.
--[p237]-- Equipment Damage
Using a damaged UPS can cause damage to the equipment in the event of instant power failure. When the battery is weak, an alarm is displayed on the screen. Replace the battery immediately after an alarm occurs.
--[p238]-- Equipment Damage
If the battery is dropped, the equipment can be damaged. Use caution when handling them.

--[p4]-- 1.3 Exception
TEL shall have no liability under this warranty and the above remedies shall not be available to the Product user (“User”) for the following damage:
(1) Damage to the Products caused by parts specified or supplied by the User.
(2) Damage to the Products caused by parts not supplied by TEL or TEL’s authorized supplier.
--[p4]-- (7) Consequential damage, including, without limitation, damage resulting from defective products (e.g. defective wafers, devices, panels and other defective products produced by using the Products) or decrease of production, caused by misuse and breakdown of the Products.
(8) Damage to the Products caused by a defect which could not have been discovered given the state of scientific or technical knowledge at the time when TEL delivered the Products.

--[p76]-- WARNING
Chemical/Electric Shock/Mechanical Drive
Unsafe conditions may be present for chemical leaks, crush hazards, and electrical shock. Lockout and tagout operations require an understanding of the necessity for lockout and tagout, an adequate understanding of the importance of lockout and tagout, and knowledge of lockout and tagout procedures. Lockout and tagout should only be performed by the trained and authorized personnel assigned to the work.

The spiral tubing is used in the process chamber to maintain good electrical conductivity between chamber parts. When chamber parts are installed or replaced, use the following procedure to inspect the spiral tubing.
--[p113]-- Purpose:
To replace the spiral tubing. Replace the spiral tubing when it has been installed incorrectly.
--[p117]-- Purpose:
O-rings are used to increase the airtightness of the load lock module, transfer module, and process module chambers. During maintenance of these modules, the O-rings should be inspected. The inspection procedure differs, based on whether or not the O-ring to be inspected is lubricated with grease. If the O-ring is damaged (scratched, cracked, and so on) or worn, it should be replaced.
--[p105]-- Purpose:
To drain the chiller coolant fluid when removing the electrostatic chuck (ESC) and to replenish the chiller coolant fluid when installing the ESC.

===== [p26-35] Strong Magnetic Field (4块) =====
--[p35]-- Strong Magnetic Field
Units equipped with magnets generate a strong magnetic field that can attract metal accessories and potentially cause bodily injury when pulled strongly to the magnets. If you wear metal accessories such as body piercing or necklace, maintain a safe distance of at least 1 m(3 ft) from units equipped with magnets.
--[p26]-- Strong Magnetic Field
Units equipped with magnets generate a strong magnetic field that can adversely affect the operation of electronic medical implanted devices such as pacemakers. People with pacemakers or other implanted medical electronic devices should maintain a safe distance of at least 1 m(3 ft) from units equipped with magnets.
--[p26]-- Strong Magnetic Field

If you wear metal accessories such as body piercing or necklace, maintain a safe distance of at least 1 m(3 ft) from units equipped with magnets.

===== [p237-237] 6.1 Replacing the Uninterruptible Power Supply (UPS) Battery <sub>16006M.20180901</sub> > Purpose: (1块) =====
--[p237]-- Purpose:
To replace the uninterruptible power supply (UPS) battery. Replace the UPS battery every three years or if the equipment alarm "UPS battery error" occurs. When the battery's power supply stops, the time-off sequence system does not work and the system cannot be turned off properly.

===== [p47-47] Lockout and Tagout > NOTE General operators, process engineers, and installation workers do not need to read this section. Equipment engineers should read this section. (2块) =====
--[p47]-- However, there is still a danger that the equipment could be started and that a major accident could occur if a third party unknowingly turns on the source of hazardous energy while maintenance operations are being performed. The purpose of lockout and tagout is to make it impossible for others to turn on the source of hazardous energy. First, the source of hazardous energy is turned off, and then a lock is put in place to make it physically impossible to turn the energy back on.
Only personnel working on the equipment can remove the lock and restore equipment operation.
--[p47]-- NOTE General operators, process engineers, and installation workers do not need to read this section. Equipment engineers should read this section.
Lockout and tagout operations are carried out before performing system maintenance to ensure safety.
To prevent the risk of injury or death, the source of hazardous energy must be turned off before beginning work on parts where there is potential danger.

===== [p146-157] 4.13 Disassembling and Reassembling the Lower Unit (Cover Ring – Insulator Ring Inner) (Type B-3) <sub>16065.20191202</sub> > Hot Surface (2块) =====
--[p157]-- Hot Surface
Contact with parts heated to temperatures above 50 °C can cause burns. Do not touch the parts in the maintenance area before verifying that the temperature is below 50 °C using a thermometer.
--[p146]-- Hot Surface
Contact with parts heated to temperatures above 50 °C can cause burns. Do not touch the parts in the maintenance area before verifying that the temperature is below 50 °C using a thermometer.

8 Confirm that the chiller main breaker is ON by checking the chiller operation screen is displayed.
9 Inform all the workers involved in the system that the maintenance operations have been completed and that the system has been returned to an operational state.

===== [p251-251] 8.2 Inspecting the Process Module Base Pressure and Leak Rate <sub>16203.20180901</sub> > Chemical (2块) =====
--[p251]-- Chemical
Exposure t o chemicals used in cleaning and maintaining t he equipment can be harmful t o your health. Wear appropriate personal protective equipment such as safety glasses and protective gloves when servicing.
Vaporized ethanol can be harmful t o your health when wiping t he t arget parts with a clean wipe moistened with ethanol.
Follow t he conditions below when working.
• The t arget part is at a normal t emperature (approx. 25 °C).
--[p251]-- • The amount of ethanol used t o moisten t he clean wipe must be 4 ml or less .
• Keep distance of at least 20 cm f rom t he wiping t arget t o your nose or mouth.
• Wait at least 30 seconds aft er wiping when moving your f ace close t o t he t arget part (to check t he conditions).
Wear a respirator f or personal protective equipment when all t he f our conditions above are not satisfied.
(Check in advance t hat t he respirator you are going t o use is a one with an appropriate ethanol canister and is appropriately managed.)

===== [p285-285] Chiller Inspection and Adjustment > Preparation Tasks (1块) =====
--[p285]-- Preparation Tasks
1 Confirm that the equipment is in an idle state and change the process module to maintenance mode (see page 94).
2 Press Temperature on the PM Maintenance screen to display the PM Temperature screen.
3 Check that Brine Temp of the bottom chiller has dropped to 40 °C or less.
4 Press Bottom Chiller on the PM Temperature screen to display the Control Bottom Chiller dialog.
5 Press Off on the Control Bottom Chiller dialog, and press OK to turn OFF the chiller to be inspected.
6 Turn OFF the chiller main breaker, and lock out (see page 72).

--[p5]-- 2.1 Liability for operation of the Products
Every operator and service person must read and thoroughly understand TEL’s manuals, including, without limitation, the operation and maintenance manuals, and any additional information provided by TEL with respect to the Products and have sufficient training by TEL concerning the operation of the Products. All Danger, Warning and Cautionary notices must be carefully read, thoroughly understood and strictly observed.
--[p5]-- The User assumes its responsibility to implement all Governmental, Federal, State and local safety regulations and standards applicable to the use of the Products.
In the event that TEL identifies that a defect in the Products may cause damage to the User, TEL will send a technology change notice to the User (“Notice”), and implement the change for the identified part of the Products without charge (“Change”).
The User will be required to make the arrangements necessary in order to complete implementation of the Change as soon as receiving the Notice.

===== [p233-234] 5.4 Cleaning the Bonnet Gate Valve Plate and Inspecting the O-ring <sub>16201.20210301</sub> > Equipment Damage (2块) =====
--[p234]-- 20 Attach the three O-rings (B) to the lifter pins.
21 Attach the two plugs to the RF plate.
22 Attach the parts from the ESC to the cover ring.
23 Lightly wipe the components and the chamber walls with a clean wipe moistened with alcohol.
24 Inspect the O-ring (see page 116) and spiral tubing (see page 112) on the chamber opening for displacement, damage or wear. If they are out of place, then press it firmly into the groove. If they are damaged or worn, replace them.
25 Close the upper unit (see page 88).
--[p233]-- Equipment Damage
Wiping the lifter pins with alcohol can cause the pins to swell, degrade lubricating ability and damage the equipment. Clean the lifter pins with a clean dry wipe.
Tighten the RF plate securing cap screws.
Torque specification: 4.5 N·m
Attach the lifter pin seal to the lifter pin and move the lifter pin seal Up and Down several times.
Confirm that the lifter pin seal moves up and down smoothly.

===== [p229-229] 5.4 Cleaning the Bonnet Gate Valve Plate and Inspecting the O-ring <sub>16201.20210301</sub> > CAUTION Chemical/Equipment Damage (1块) =====
--[p229]-- CAUTION Chemical/Equipment Damage
When draining the chiller coolant fluid, wear personal protective equipment and protective gloves (chemically resistant), and follow the instructions in Extracting and Replenishing Chiller Coolant Fluid.
7 Lock out the chiller main breaker (see page 72).
8 Turn OFF and lock out the sources of energy in the following order:
Process module air supply (see page 66)
• Gas lines (see page 52)
• Process module power supply (see page 55)
9 Set up a step stool.

===== [p56-56] 2.7 Locking Out and Tagging Out the Process Module Power Supply <sub>15900F-632.20210301</sub> > Main Tasks (1块) =====
--[p56]-- Main Tasks
1 Inform all workers involved with the system that maintenance is required and that the process module power supply must be locked out and tagged out.
2 If the process module is operating, change the process module to maintenance mode (see page 94).
3 Confirm the status of the following LEDs and lamps.
• LED for the ESC HV power supply is ON
• LED for the HF RF generator is ON
• Thyristor power supply lamp in the ATCC heater controller box is ON
• LED for the LF RF generator is ON

===== [p40-40] 6 Example of Model Numbers and Abbreviations: 3WWZZWW (1块) =====
--[p40 [表]]-- 6 Example of Model Numbers and Abbreviations: 3WWZZWW
Number | Alphabet
The first digit indicates the wafer size. 3: 12 inches or 300 mm 2: 8 inches or 200 mm | Alphabets indicate the chamber type and numbers indicate the number of chamber connection. W: Vigus Z: Without chamber
